    # Draw.io Diagram Skill
    
    Generate draw.io diagrams as native `.drawio` files. Author each diagram either as **Mermaid** (concise text that the draw.io desktop CLI converts and lays out for you) or as **draw.io XML** directly. Optionally auto-layout XML-authored diagrams with **ELK**, export to PNG/SVG/PDF with the diagram XML embedded (so the exported file stays editable in draw.io), or generate a browser URL that opens the diagram directly in the draw.io editor.
    
    **Inline viewer first.** This skill writes files and opens them with a shell command. If the draw.io MCP server's `create_diagram` tool is available in a chat that shows diagrams inline (VS Code's or Cursor's own chat, Claude.ai, ChatGPT), and the user hasn't asked for a `.drawio` file, an export or a URL, call `create_diagram` instead: the diagram appears in the conversation, with no file and no shell command. Claude Code, Codex and Copilot CLI don't show diagrams inline, not even in their VS Code or JetBrains extensions: there `create_diagram` only returns a link, so use this skill.
    
    ## Authoring: Mermaid or XML?
    
    The desktop CLI converts Mermaid to a native `.drawio` file and lays it out automatically, which is far more reliable than hand-positioning cells in XML.
    
    | Author as | Best for | Needs desktop CLI? |
    |-----------|----------|--------------------|
    | **Mermaid** | Flowcharts, sequence, class, state, ER, gantt, mindmap, timeline, user journey, quadrant, C4, git graph, pie, and other standard types | Yes — to convert to `.drawio` |
    | **XML** | Custom styling, precise/hand positioning, specific shape libraries (AWS, Azure, network, UML detail…), or when the desktop CLI is not installed | No (optional ELK `--layout` needs the CLI) |
    
    - **Prefer Mermaid** when the desktop CLI is available and the request is one of the standard types above.
    - **Use XML** for precise control, or as the universal fallback: XML needs no CLI, so it's the only option when the desktop app isn't installed (deliver a `.drawio` file or a `url`). For XML you can let the CLI apply an [ELK auto-layout](#elk-layout-for-xml) instead of computing coordinates yourself.
    
    If you're unsure whether the desktop CLI is present, detect it first (see [Locating the CLI](#locating-the-cli)).
    
    ## The pipeline
    
    Every diagram becomes a native `.drawio` file first, then is delivered in the requested output format, whether you authored Mermaid or XML.
    
    1. **Author → `.drawio`**
       - **Mermaid**: write the Mermaid to a `.mmd` file, then convert it with the CLI:
         ```bash
         drawio -x -f xml -o diagram.drawio diagram.mmd
         ```
         Delete the `.mmd` afterward — the `.drawio` is the artifact.
       - **XML**: write the mxGraphModel XML to `diagram.drawio` (see [XML format](#xml-format)), then run the [repair pass](#repair-pass) on it. Optionally apply an [ELK layout](#elk-layout-for-xml).
    2. **Deliver** (identical for both sources):
       - *(no format)* → keep `diagram.drawio` and open it.
       - **png / svg / pdf** → export from the `.drawio` with embedded XML, then delete the source `.drawio`:
         ```bash
         drawio -x -f png -e -b 10 -o diagram.drawio.png diagram.drawio
         ```
       - **url** → build a browser URL from the `.drawio` XML, open it, and keep the `.drawio` as a local copy (see [Browser URL output](#browser-url-output)).
    3. **Open the result** — the exported file, the URL, or the `.drawio` (see [Opening the result](#opening-the-result)). If the open command fails, print the absolute path (or URL) so the user can open it manually.
    
    **Always convert Mermaid to `.drawio` first, then export** — direct Mermaid → PNG export with `-e` is broken in current draw.io Desktop (the embedded-XML step crashes); the two-step path is reliable and produces an editable embed.
    
    If Mermaid was requested but no desktop CLI is available, author the same diagram as XML.
    
    ## ELK layout for XML
    
    The CLI's `--layout` step auto-positions XML-authored diagrams with the same ELK layouts as the editor's *Arrange ▸ Layout* menu and the draw.io MCP app server. Generate the cells with approximate (or even `0,0`) positions and get the graph *structure* — nodes and edges — right. Use `verticalFlow` / `horizontalFlow` for flow-style diagrams and `organic` for networks. Mermaid-authored diagrams are already laid out — don't add `--layout`.
    
    Lay out in place after you write the file (reading and overwriting the same path is supported), or combine layout with export in one call:
    
    ```bash
    drawio -x -f xml --layout verticalFlow -o diagram.drawio diagram.drawio
    drawio -x -f png -e -b 10 --layout verticalFlow -o diagram.drawio.png diagram.drawio
    ```
    
    ### Layout presets
    
    | Name | Layout |
    |------|--------|
    | `verticalFlow` | Layered, top-to-bottom — flowcharts, pipelines |
    | `horizontalFlow` | Layered, left-to-right |
    | `verticalTree` | Tree, top-down — hierarchies, org charts |
    | `horizontalTree` | Tree, left-to-right |
    | `radialTree` | Radial tree |
    | `organic` | Force-directed — networks, mind-map-like graphs |
    
    ### Custom layout JSON
    
    For finer control, pass a JSON **array** (starting with `[`) instead of a preset name — the format of the editor's custom-layout dialog:
    
    ```bash
    drawio -x -f xml --layout '[{"layout":"elkLayered","config":{"elk.direction":"RIGHT"}}]' -o diagram.drawio diagram.drawio
    ```
    
    Each entry is `{"layout": <algorithm>, "config": { … }}`. Algorithms: `elkLayered`, `elkTree`, `elkRadial`, `elkOrganic`, `elkStress`, `elkBox`. Config entries starting with `elk.` are ELK options, e.g. `elk.direction` (`UP` / `DOWN` / `LEFT` / `RIGHT`), `elk.spacing.nodeNode`, `elk.layered.spacing.nodeNodeBetweenLayers`; `edgeStyle` (e.g. `orthogonal`) and `corners` (e.g. `rounded`) control connector rendering.
    
    ### Orthogonal edge routing
    
    `--layout libavoid` routes the **edges** orthogonally around the shapes (*Arrange ▸ Layout ▸ Orthogonal Routing*) without moving any vertex. Run it in place on hand-positioned XML whose connectors cross shapes; skip it after a flow/tree preset, which already routes its edges:
    
    ```bash
    drawio -x -f xml --layout libavoid -o diagram.drawio diagram.drawio
    ```
    
    ## Mermaid syntax reference
    
    When authoring Mermaid, fetch and follow the shared Mermaid reference (all supported diagram types plus flowchart styling — `style`, `classDef`, `linkStyle`):
    
    https://raw.githubusercontent.com/jgraph/drawio-mcp/main/shared/mermaid-reference.md
    
    Match the language of the diagram labels to the user's language.
    
    ## Choosing the output format
    
    Check the user's request for a format preference. Examples:
    
    - `/drawio:drawio create a flowchart` → Mermaid → `flowchart.drawio`
    - `/drawio:drawio png flowchart for login` → Mermaid → `login-flow.drawio.png`
    - `/drawio:drawio svg: ER diagram` → Mermaid → `er-diagram.drawio.svg`
    - `/drawio:drawio pdf AWS architecture overview` → XML (needs AWS shapes) → `architecture-overview.drawio.pdf`
    - `/drawio:drawio url flowchart for user login` → opens browser at `app.diagrams.net` with the diagram, keeps `login-flow.drawio` locally
    
    If no format is mentioned, produce the `.drawio` file and open it; the user can ask to export later.
    
    PNG, SVG and PDF are exported with `-e` (`--embed-diagram`): the file contains the full diagram XML, so opening it in draw.io recovers the editable diagram. `jpg` is lossy and can't embed the XML.
    
    ## Browser URL output
    
    For `url` format, keep the `.drawio` file on disk as the user's local copy, compress its XML with Node.js's built-in `zlib` into a `https://app.diagrams.net/#R...` URL, and open that in the default browser — no draw.io Desktop needed to *view* it.
    
    ### URL generation
    
    Run this `node -e` one-liner to read the `.drawio` file and print the URL (replace `DIAGRAM.drawio` with the actual filename):
    
    ```bash
    URL=$(node -e '
    const fs = require("fs");
    const zlib = require("zlib");
    const xml = fs.readFileSync(process.argv[1], "utf8");
    const compressed = zlib.deflateRawSync(encodeURIComponent(xml)).toString("base64");
    console.log("https://app.diagrams.net/?grid=0&pv=0&border=10&edit=_blank#R" + encodeURIComponent(compressed));
    ' "DIAGRAM.drawio")
    ```
    
    This is the MCP Tool Server's XML link format. Don't use a `#create=` URL for XML: draw.io rewrites that hash after loading it, and when the rewrite differs from the link (it does for Node's `zlib` output, which isn't byte-identical to draw.io's pako) it asks "All changes will be lost!".
    
    ### Opening the URL
    
    | Environment | Command |
    |-------------|---------|
    | macOS | `open "$URL"` |
    | Linux (native) | `xdg-open "$URL"` |
    | WSL2 | Write a temp `.url` file, open via `cmd.exe` (see below) |
    | Windows (native) | Write a temp `.url` file, open via `start` (see below) |
    
    `cmd.exe`'s `start` command treats `&` as a command separator and strips everything after `#`, which is where the diagram lives, so on Windows and WSL2 put the URL in a `.url` shortcut file instead.
    
    **WSL2:**
    
    ```bash
    TMPFILE=$(mktemp --suffix=.url)
    printf '[InternetShortcut]\r\nURL=%s\r\n' "$URL" > "$TMPFILE"
    cmd.exe /c start "" "$(wslpath -w "$TMPFILE")"
    ```
    
    **Windows (native):** do **not** build the `.url` file with `echo URL=%URL%` — the `&` characters (`?grid=0&pv=0&...`) cut the shortcut short and the diagram is lost. Let Node write the file and open only its path, which never contains `&`:
    
    ```bash
    TMPFILE=$(node -e '
    const fs = require("fs");
    const os = require("os");
    const path = require("path");
    const p = path.join(os.tmpdir(), "drawio.url");
    fs.writeFileSync(p, "[InternetShortcut]\r\nURL=" + process.argv[1] + "\r\n");
    process.stdout.write(p);
    ' "$URL")
    cmd.exe /c start "" "$TMPFILE"
    ```
    
    Then print the URL and the local file so the user can share or re-edit it:
    
    ```
    Opened in browser: <URL>
    Local file: DIAGRAM.drawio
    ```
    
    Very large diagrams may exceed the browser's URL length limit (roughly 32K–2MB depending on the browser); then open the `.drawio` file locally instead.
    
    ## draw.io CLI
    
    The draw.io desktop app's command-line interface **converts Mermaid** to `.drawio`, applies **ELK layouts** (`--layout`), and **exports** to PNG/SVG/PDF. All three need the desktop app installed.
    
    ### Locating the CLI
    
    Detect the environment first, then locate the CLI. On Windows (native and WSL2) the installer does **not** put draw.io on PATH and the install directory is user-selectable, so work through the whole fallback chain before concluding the CLI is absent.
    
    #### WSL2 (Windows Subsystem for Linux)
    
    WSL2 is detected when `/proc/version` contains `microsoft` or `WSL`:
    
    ```bash
    grep -qi microsoft /proc/version 2>/dev/null && echo "WSL2"
    ```
    
    On WSL2, use the Windows draw.io Desktop executable via `/mnt/c/...`:
    
    ```bash
    DRAWIO_CMD="/mnt/c/Program Files/draw.io/draw.io.exe"
    ```
    
    Double-quote the path so the space in `Program Files` stays part of it. Do **not** wrap it in backticks — in bash that is command substitution, which would *execute* the binary instead of storing its path.
    
    If draw.io is installed in a non-default location, check common alternatives:
    
    ```bash
    # Default install path
    "/mnt/c/Program Files/draw.io/draw.io.exe"
    
    # Per-user install (if the above does not exist)
    "/mnt/c/Users/$WIN_USER/AppData/Local/Programs/draw.io/draw.io.exe"
    ```
    
    If neither exists, the install is on another drive or in a custom directory — scan the other mounted drives, then ask the registry:
    
    ```bash
    # Program Files on the other drives (D:, E:, …)
    ls /mnt/*/Program\ Files/draw.io/draw.io.exe 2>/dev/null | head -1
    
    # Registry — the installer records the directory the user picked
    for REG_PATH in 'HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall' \
                    'HKLM\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall' \
                    'HKLM\Software\WOW6432Node\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall'; do
      WIN_DIR=$(reg.exe query "$REG_PATH" /s /v InstallLocation 2>/dev/null |
                grep -i 'REG_SZ.*draw\.io' | head -1 |
                sed 's/.*REG_SZ[[:space:]]*//' | tr -d '\r')
      [ -n "$WIN_DIR" ] && DRAWIO_CMD="$(wslpath -u "${WIN_DIR%\\}")/draw.io.exe" && break
    done
    ```
    
    #### macOS and Linux
    
    - **Linux (native)**: `drawio`, typically on PATH via snap/apt/flatpak — confirm with `which drawio`
    - **macOS**: this prints the first of `drawio` on PATH (Homebrew), `/Applications`, the per-user `~/Applications` and Spotlight, or nothing if draw.io is absent:
    
    ```bash
    { command -v drawio; ls /Applications/draw.io.app/Contents/MacOS/draw.io ~/Applications/draw.io.app/Contents/MacOS/draw.io
      mdfind "kMDItemCFBundleIdentifier == 'com.jgraph.drawio.desktop'" | sed 's|$|/Contents/MacOS/draw.io|'; } 2>/dev/null | head -1
    ```
    
    Call the binary inside the `.app`: a copy or symlink outside it dies with `Unable to find helper app`.
    
    #### Windows (native, non-WSL2)
    
    Check, in order — a missing PATH entry or a non-`C:` install drive is normal, not a sign that draw.io is missing:
    
    1. **PATH**: `where.exe draw.io` (note the dot — the executable is `draw.io.exe`, not `drawio.exe`)
    2. **Default install paths**: `C:\Program Files\draw.io\draw.io.exe`, then the per-user install `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\draw.io\draw.io.exe`
    3. **The registry** uninstall entries, which record the directory the user picked
    4. **The other drives**: `Program Files\draw.io\draw.io.exe` on `D:`, `E:`, …
    
    Save this as `find-drawio.ps1` to run the whole chain at once — it prints the first executable it finds, and nothing at all if draw.io really is not installed:
    
    ```powershell
    $c = @()
    $c += (where.exe draw.io 2>$null)
    $c += "$env:ProgramFiles\draw.io\draw.io.exe", "$env:LOCALAPPDATA\Programs\draw.io\draw.io.exe"
    $c += Get-ItemProperty 'HKCU:\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall\*',
                           'HKLM:\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall\*',
                           'HKLM:\Software\WOW6432Node\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall\*' -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
          Where-Object { $_.DisplayName -like '*draw.io*' } |
          ForEach-Object { if ($_.InstallLocation) { Join-Path $_.InstallLocation 'draw.io.exe' } else { ($_.DisplayIcon -split ',')[0] } }
    $c += (Get-PSDrive -PSProvider FileSystem).Root | ForEach-Object { Join-Path $_ 'Program Files\draw.io\draw.io.exe' }
    $c | Where-Object { $_ -and (Test-Path $_) } | Select-Object -First 1
    ```
    
    From a bash or cmd shell, run it with:
    
    ```bash
    powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File find-drawio.ps1
    ```
    
    Quote the resulting path on every call — it almost always contains spaces:
    
    ```
    & "D:\Program Files\draw.io\draw.io.exe" -x -f xml -o diagram.drawio diagram.mmd
    ```
    
    Only once every step comes up empty should you treat the CLI as absent and fall back to XML authoring with `.drawio` / `url` output. When you find it outside PATH, mention that adding its folder (e.g. `D:\Program Files\draw.io`) to PATH makes it discoverable next time.
    
    ### Export command
    
    ```bash
    drawio -x -f <format> -e -b 10 -o "<output>" "<input.drawio>"
    ```
    
    On WSL2, call the Windows executable, e.g. `"/mnt/c/Program Files/draw.io/draw.io.exe" -x -f png -e -b 10 -o "diagram.drawio.png" "diagram.drawio"`. The convert and layout commands are in [The pipeline](#the-pipeline) and [ELK layout for XML](#elk-layout-for-xml).
    
    Key flags:
    - `-x` / `--export`: export mode (also used for Mermaid conversion and layouts)
    - `-f` / `--format`: output format (`xml`, png, svg, pdf, jpg) — use `xml` to produce a `.drawio` from Mermaid or a layout
    - `--layout`: run a layout before writing the output — an ELK preset name, `libavoid` edge routing, or a custom-layout JSON array
    - `--mermaid-image 1`: convert Mermaid to a single static SVG image cell (the Mermaid source stays on the cell for re-editing) instead of an editable diagram — only when the user explicitly asks for a non-editable image cell
    - `-e` / `--embed-diagram`: embed diagram XML in the output (PNG, SVG, PDF only)
    - `-o` / `--output`: output file path
    - `-b` / `--border`: border width around diagram (default: 0)
    - `-t` / `--transparent`: transparent background (PNG only)
    - `-s` / `--scale`: scale the diagram size
    - `--width` / `--height`: fit into specified dimensions (preserves aspect ratio)
    - `-a` / `--all-pages`: export all pages (PDF only)
    - `-p` / `--page-index`: select a specific page (1-based)
    - `--disable-gpu`: skip GPU initialisation — add it when a command dies with `GPU process isn't usable` (Windows remote-desktop sessions, VMs, CI runners); no convert, layout, or export path needs the GPU
    
    ### Opening the result
    
    **Inside VS Code** (also Cursor or Windsurf, with their `cursor` / `windsurf` commands in place of `code`), open the file in an editor tab, next to the chat: with the [Draw.io Integration](https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=hediet.vscode-drawio) extension installed, VS Code shows `.drawio`, `.drawio.svg` and `.drawio.png` files as editable diagrams. One command checks for the extension and otherwise falls back to the platform's command below (`open` on macOS):
    
    ```bash
    code --list-extensions 2>/dev/null | grep -qi '^hediet.vscode-drawio$' && code <file> || open <file>
    ```
    
    PDFs and `url` mode always use the platform's command:
    
    | Environment | Command |
    |-------------|---------|
    | macOS | `open <file>` |
    | Linux (native) | `xdg-open <file>` |
    | WSL2 | `cmd.exe /c start "" "$(wslpath -w <file>)"` |
    | Windows | `start <file>` |
    
    On WSL2, `wslpath -w` turns the path into a Windows path, since `cmd.exe` can't resolve `/mnt/c/...`, and the empty `""` keeps `start` from reading the filename as a window title.
    
    ## File naming
    
    - Use a descriptive, lowercase, hyphenated name based on the content (e.g., `login-flow`, `database-schema`)
    - Exports use double extensions — `name.drawio.png`, `name.drawio.svg`, `name.drawio.pdf` — to signal the embedded diagram XML
    - For `url` mode, keep the `.drawio` file (no double extension) — the URL is a view/edit handle and the local file is the persistent copy
    
    ## XML format
    
    A `.drawio` file is native mxGraphModel XML, the same format the CLI converts Mermaid to.
    
    ### Basic structure
    
    Every diagram must have this structure:
    
    ```xml
    <mxGraphModel adaptiveColors="auto">
      <root>
        <mxCell id="0"/>
        <mxCell id="1" parent="0"/>
      </root>
    </mxGraphModel>
    ```
    
    - Cell `id="0"` is the root layer
    - Cell `id="1"` is the default parent layer
    - All diagram elements use `parent="1"` unless using multiple layers
    - **Cells inside a container** use `parent="<container_id>"` and coordinates relative to that container
    - **Edges belong to the innermost container that holds BOTH endpoints** — same container (at any nesting depth) → that container's id; one end outside every container → `parent="1"`. An auto-layout reads an edge's coordinates in its parent's frame, so an edge parked further out than its endpoints is laid out in the wrong place
    
    ### CRITICAL: XML well-formedness
    
    - **NEVER include ANY XML comments (`<!-- -->`) in the output.** XML comments are strictly forbidden — they waste tokens, can cause parse errors, and serve no purpose in diagram XML.
    - Escape special characters in attribute values: `&amp;`, `&lt;`, `&gt;`, `&quot;`
    - Always use unique `id` values for each `mxCell`
    - Every edge needs a child `<mxGeometry relative="1" as="geometry" />` — a self-closing edge `mxCell` doesn't render
    
    ### Repair pass
    
    Run this right after writing XML to a `.drawio` file, before any layout, export or URL step. It fixes the mechanical slips that make draw.io reject a diagram: text around the diagram (a code fence, a sentence, a stray closing tag), `<` or a stray `&` inside an attribute value (an unescaped HTML label, "R&D", `&nbsp;`), and the default layer written as its own parent (`<mxCell id="1" parent="1"/>`, which hides everything). It changes nothing in a well-formed file, and needs only Node.js (skip it if `node` isn't installed). Replace `DIAGRAM.drawio` with the actual filename:
    
    ```bash
    node -e '
    const fs = require("fs");
    const file = process.argv[1];
    let xml = fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8");
    const diagram = xml.match(/<mxfile[\s\S]*?<\/mxfile>|<mxGraphModel[\s\S]*?<\/mxGraphModel>/);
    if (diagram && xml.trim() !== diagram[0]) xml = diagram[0];
    xml = xml.replace(/="([^"]*)"/g, function(m, v)
    {
      return "=\"" + v.replace(/</g, "&lt;").replace(/&(?!(?:amp|lt|gt|quot|apos|#[0-9]+|#x[0-9a-fA-F]+);)/g, "&amp;") + "\"";
    });
    xml = xml.replace(/<mxCell id="([^"]+)" parent="\1"\s*\/>/g, "<mxCell id=\"$1\" parent=\"0\"/>");
    fs.writeFileSync(file, xml);
    ' "DIAGRAM.drawio"
    ```
    
    ## XML reference
    
    Before authoring XML, fetch and follow the condensed XML rules — skeleton, vertex and edge templates, labels, shapes, the layout grid, containers, swimlanes and edge styles:
    https://raw.githubusercontent.com/jgraph/drawio-mcp/main/shared/xml-essentials.md
    
    They cover most diagrams. Only when a diagram needs layers, tags, metadata and placeholders, tables, cross-functional grids or dark-mode colors, also fetch the full XML reference (it describes the draw.io MCP servers: for their `search_shapes` tool use the [lookup below](#shapes-and-icons), for `postLayout` / `routing` the CLI's [`--layout`](#elk-layout-for-xml)):
    https://raw.githubusercontent.com/jgraph/drawio-mcp/main/shared/xml-reference.md
    
    ### Shapes and icons
    
    When a diagram needs cloud, network or brand icons (AWS, Azure, GCP, Cisco, Kubernetes, GitHub, Slack…), never guess `mxgraph.*` names: look them all up in one call to `scripts/find-shapes.js` in this skill's folder (Node.js 18+, network), one query per icon, narrowed by library (`aws4`, `azure2`, `cisco19`). It searches draw.io's shape libraries, then its icon service, and prints `title width×height style` lines; use a style as is:
    
    ```bash
    node <skill-folder>/scripts/find-shapes.js "aws4 lambda" "github" "slack"
    ```
    
    ## Troubleshooting
    
    | Problem | Cause | Solution |
    |---------|-------|----------|
    | draw.io CLI not found | Desktop app not installed or not on PATH | Work through [Locating the CLI](#locating-the-cli); if it's really absent, author as XML and deliver a `.drawio` file or `url`, and tell the user that installing the draw.io desktop app enables Mermaid conversion, layouts and image export |
    | Mermaid → PNG export crashes | Direct `.mmd` → PNG with `-e` is broken in current draw.io Desktop | Convert Mermaid to `.drawio` first (`-f xml`), then export the `.drawio` |
    | Blank diagram from Mermaid | Misspelled type keyword, or a syntax error (bad node ID, unquoted label) | Check the [Mermaid reference](#mermaid-syntax-reference); the first non-directive line's keyword selects the diagram type |
    | Layout does nothing / errors | Unknown preset name, custom JSON not an array, or a desktop build too old for `--layout` / `.mmd` input | Use a [preset](#layout-presets) or a JSON array starting with `[`; on an old desktop build, author as XML with explicit positions and tell the user updating draw.io Desktop enables Mermaid conversion and layouts |
    | Export produces empty/corrupt file, or the diagram looks blank | Invalid XML, or missing root cells `id="0"` and `id="1"` | Run the [repair pass](#repair-pass) and check the [basic structure](#basic-structure) |
    | `GPU process isn't usable. Goodbye.` during convert/layout/export | Electron cannot start a GPU process — typical on Windows remote-desktop sessions, VMs, and CI runners. The `os_crypt` / `Failed to decrypt` lines printed before it are unrelated noise | Retry the same command with `--disable-gpu` added; draw.io Desktop's own `--disable-acceleration` switch has the same effect |
    | Browser opens with an empty diagram in `url` mode | `cmd.exe` stripped the `#R...` fragment | Use the `.url` file workaround on Windows/WSL2 (see [Opening the URL](#opening-the-url)) — never pass the URL directly to `cmd.exe /c start` |

    $ python - <<'PY'
        import xml.etree.ElementTree as E, re, collections
        p='system-context-diagram.drawio.xml'
        r=E.parse(p).getroot()
        print('XML valid;', r.tag, r.attrib)
        for d in r.findall('diagram'):
         print('\nPAGE', d.get('name'),d.get('id'))
         cells=d.findall('.//mxCell'); objs=d.findall('.//object'); ids=[x.get('id') for x in d.iter() if x.get('id')]; names={o.get('id'):o.get('c4Name',o.get('c4Description','')) for o in objs}
         names.update({c.get('id'):re.sub('<[^>]*>',' ',c.get('value','')) for c in cells if c.get('id')})
         print('vertices',sum(c.get('vertex')=='1' for c in cells),'edges',sum(c.get('edge')=='1' for c in cells))
         for o in objs:
          c=o.find('mxCell')
          if c is not None: print(o.get('c4Type'),o.get('c4Name',''),o.get('c4Technology',''),o.get('c4Description',''), (' : '+names.get(c.get('source'),str(c.get('source')))+' -> '+names.get(c.get('target'),str(c.get('target')))) if c.get('edge')=='1' else '')
         if not objs:
          for c in cells:
           if c.get('value'): print('LABEL',names[c.get('id')])
         for c in cells:
          for k in ('parent','source','target'):
           if c.get(k) and c.get(k) not in ids: print('BROKEN',k,c.get(k))
         print('duplicate ids',[i for i,n in collections.Counter(ids).items() if n>1])

    ## Preserve the C4 Context legend
    
    When editing `system-context-diagram.drawio.xml`, leave the entire Legend group
    on the `C4 Context` page unchanged unless the user explicitly requests a change
    to that legend.

      the legend are intentional examples. Do not flag them as unfinished model
      content or replace them.

      legend as part of other diagram edits.

Using the existing legend’s Person, Software System, and Relationship styles, the initial context would be:

| Element | Proposed description / relationship |
|---|---|
| **Player** | Participates in Normal and Imposter games, draws, guesses, and views results. |
| **Host** | A player who creates rooms, configures the mode and settings, and starts games. |
| **Draw & Guess Game** | A multiplayer system supporting real-time drawing, guessing, room management, and scoring in Normal and Imposter modes. |
| **Player → Game** | “Joins rooms, plays drawing and guessing rounds, and views results.” |
| **Host → Game** | “Creates rooms, selects game modes, configures settings, and starts games.” |

I’d treat **Imposter** as a temporary gameplay role, rather than a separate context actor, unless that role interacts with the system in a substantially different way.

Before we settle the design:

1. **How does Imposter mode work?** Does one player receive no word or a different word, and do players vote to identify them?
2. **Can people play as guests**, or must they register and log in?
3. **What can the host control?** For example: mode, word category, round count, timer, kicking players.
4. **Are there external services** already planned, such as Google sign-in or email delivery? We should show only confirmed integrations.

## Assistant

Based on your answers, I propose **two people and two software systems**, using the existing C4 Context legend.

| Element | Description |
|---|---|
| **Player** | A registered user who plays Normal and Imposter modes, draws, guesses, votes when applicable, and views results. |
| **Host** | A player who creates a room and controls the keyword. |
| **Draw & Guess Game** | An online multiplayer system that manages accounts, rooms, real-time drawing, Normal and Imposter gameplay, voting, and results. |
| **Google Identity** — external system | Authenticates users who choose Google sign-in. |

The relationships would be:

| From → To | Label |
|---|---|
| Player → Game | Registers, signs in, joins rooms, plays games, and views results |
| Host → Game | Creates rooms and controls the keyword |
| Game → Google Identity | Authenticates users through Google sign-in |

The **Imposter remains a Player** assigned a hidden role during a match. The keyword distribution, drawing turns, voting, reveal, and winning-side calculation stay inside the Game system.

One gameplay decision needs resolving: **if the host chooses the keyword, can the host also become the imposter?** They would already know the answer. Possible rules are:

- The host chooses the keyword and is excluded from being the imposter.
- The host selects a keyword pool; the system secretly chooses the actual keyword, so the host can also become the imposter.

Also, does Normal mode use **one drawer while everyone else guesses**, and does the imposter draw on their turn to blend in?

No file changes yet. Once we settle these rules and you approve the design, I’ll update the context page while preserving the legend exactly.

    # C4 Context design log
    
    Date: 2026-11-07
    
    Status: Proposed design recorded for review. Approval to apply changes to the
    draw.io file is still pending; this log does not authorize diagram edits.
    
    Target file: `system-context-diagram.drawio.xml`
    Target page: `C4 Context`
    Proposed title: **Draw & Guess Game — System Context**
    
    ## Confirmed requirements
    
    - The multiplayer Draw & Guess Game supports Normal and Imposter modes.
    - Users must register and sign in before playing.
    - Google sign-in is supported.
    - Keywords come from the system's default pool, with the system selecting the
      keyword instead of the host supplying it.
    - In Imposter mode, there are n players. All except the imposter know the keyword.
      Non-imposters draw strokes representing parts of the keyword on their turns.
      The imposter tries to infer the keyword and blend in. Players vote to identify
      the imposter, who is then revealed, and the system determines the winning side.
    
    ## Proposed context elements
    
    | Element | Type | Description |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | Player | Person | A registered user who joins rooms, plays Normal and Imposter modes, draws, guesses, votes when applicable, and views results. |
    | Host | Person | A registered player who creates rooms, selects the game mode, and starts games. Also participates as a player. |
    | Draw & Guess Game | Software System | An online multiplayer game that manages accounts, rooms, real-time drawing and guessing, Normal and Imposter modes, voting, scoring, and results. |
    | Google Identity | External Software System | Provides Google sign-in to authenticate users. |
    
    Host responsibilities above are proposed behavior, pending design approval.
    Host is a Player role; the separate actor highlights room-hosting interactions.
    No UML inheritance arrow is proposed for this context diagram.
    
    ## Proposed relationships
    
    | From | To | Label |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | Player | Draw & Guess Game | Registers, signs in, joins rooms, plays games, and views results |
    | Host | Draw & Guess Game | Creates rooms, selects the game mode, and starts games |
    | Player | Google Identity | Signs in with Google |
    | Draw & Guess Game | Google Identity | Uses Google to authenticate users |
    
    The Host also has Player interactions, including Google sign-in. A duplicate
    Host-to-Google relationship is omitted for readability.
    
    ## Scope and layout
    
    - Keep Normal and Imposter modes inside the Game system, rather than drawing
      them as separate software systems.
    - Keep the keyword pool, hidden-role assignment, keyword distribution, drawing
      turns, voting, imposter reveal, scoring, and win determination inside the Game.
    - System-selected keywords allow the host to be eligible for the imposter role.
    - Place Player and Host above the Game, with Google Identity to the right.
    - Use the existing legend's Person, blue Software System, gray external Software
      System, and Relationship styles.
    - Replace the obsolete `Status Dashboard (Context)` title and actual-model
      relationship placeholders. Do not replace example labels in the legend.
    - Position the actual diagram elements within the page without moving the legend.
    - Limit eventual diagram edits to the C4 Context page.
    
    ## Legend preservation
    
    Follow `AGENTS.md`. Leave the complete Legend group
    `GGAcaCNyiWMHBxoHMokF-1` and all descendants unchanged, including labels,
    attributes, styles, geometry, and edges, unless the user explicitly requests
    changes to the legend. Its placeholder labels are intentional notation examples.
    
    ## Gameplay details still unspecified
    
    These details do not block the proposed system context, but must not be invented
    in later detailed designs:
    
    - Normal mode's exact turn structure and scoring rules.
    - Whether the imposter draws on their own turn.
    - Voting ties and exact winning-side conditions.
    
    ## Application record
    
    Only this Markdown log has been added for this request. The proposed diagram
    changes have not been applied.

    | Player | Person | A registered user who joins rooms, plays Normal and Imposter modes, draws, guesses, votes when applicable, and views results. |
    | Host | Person | A registered player who creates rooms, selects the game mode, and starts games. Also participates as a player. |
    | Draw & Guess Game | Software System | An online multiplayer game that manages accounts, rooms, real-time drawing and guessing, Normal and Imposter modes, voting, scoring, and results. |
    | Google Identity | External Software System | Provides Google sign-in to authenticate users. |
    
    Host responsibilities above are proposed behavior, pending design approval.
    Host is a Player role; the separate actor highlights room-hosting interactions.
    No UML inheritance arrow is proposed for this context diagram.
    
    ## Proposed relationships
    
    | From | To | Label |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | Player | Draw & Guess Game | Registers, signs in, joins rooms, plays games, and views results |
    | Host | Draw & Guess Game | Creates rooms, selects the game mode, and starts games |
    | Player | Google Identity | Signs in with Google |
    | Draw & Guess Game | Google Identity | Uses Google to authenticate users |
    
    The Host also has Player interactions, including Google sign-in. A duplicate
    Host-to-Google relationship is omitted for readability.
    
    ## Scope and layout
    
    - Keep Normal and Imposter modes inside the Game system, rather than drawing
      them as separate software systems.
    - Keep the keyword pool, hidden-role assignment, keyword distribution, drawing
      turns, voting, imposter reveal, scoring, and win determination inside the Game.
    - System-selected keywords allow the host to be eligible for the imposter role.
    - Place Player and Host above the Game, with Google Identity to the right.
    - Use the existing legend's Person, blue Software System, gray external Software
      System, and Relationship styles.
    - Replace the obsolete `Status Dashboard (Context)` title and actual-model
      relationship placeholders. Do not replace example labels in the legend.
    - Position the actual diagram elements within the page without moving the legend.
    - Limit eventual diagram edits to the C4 Context page.
    
    ## Legend preservation

    `GGAcaCNyiWMHBxoHMokF-1` and all descendants unchanged, including labels,
    attributes, styles, geometry, and edges, unless the user explicitly requests

    ## Gameplay details still unspecified

    in later detailed designs:

    - Whether the imposter draws on their own turn.
    - Voting ties and exact winning-side conditions.

    | `GPU process isn't usable. Goodbye.` during convert/layout/export | Electron cannot start a GPU process — typical on Windows remote-desktop sessions, VMs, and CI runners. The `os_crypt` / `Failed to decrypt` lines printed before it are unrelated noise | Retry the same command with `--disable-gpu` added; draw.io Desktop's own `--disable-acceleration` switch has the same effect |
    | Browser opens with an empty diagram in `url` mode | `cmd.exe` stripped the `#R...` fragment | Use the `.url` file workaround on Windows/WSL2 (see [Opening the URL](#opening-the-url)) — never pass the URL directly to `cmd.exe /c start` |

## Activity

    $ sed -n '340,390p' /home/cookies/.codex/plugins/cache/drawio/drawio/1.2.6/skills/drawio/SKILL.md
    - Cell `id="0"` is the root layer
    - Cell `id="1"` is the default parent layer
    - All diagram elements use `parent="1"` unless using multiple layers
    - **Cells inside a container** use `parent="<container_id>"` and coordinates relative to that container
    - **Edges belong to the innermost container that holds BOTH endpoints** — same container (at any nesting depth) → that container's id; one end outside every container → `parent="1"`. An auto-layout reads an edge's coordinates in its parent's frame, so an edge parked further out than its endpoints is laid out in the wrong place
    
    ### CRITICAL: XML well-formedness
    
    - **NEVER include ANY XML comments (`<!-- -->`) in the output.** XML comments are strictly forbidden — they waste tokens, can cause parse errors, and serve no purpose in diagram XML.
    - Escape special characters in attribute values: `&amp;`, `&lt;`, `&gt;`, `&quot;`
    - Always use unique `id` values for each `mxCell`
    - Every edge needs a child `<mxGeometry relative="1" as="geometry" />` — a self-closing edge `mxCell` doesn't render
    
    ### Repair pass
    
    Run this right after writing XML to a `.drawio` file, before any layout, export or URL step. It fixes the mechanical slips that make draw.io reject a diagram: text around the diagram (a code fence, a sentence, a stray closing tag), `<` or a stray `&` inside an attribute value (an unescaped HTML label, "R&D", `&nbsp;`), and the default layer written as its own parent (`<mxCell id="1" parent="1"/>`, which hides everything). It changes nothing in a well-formed file, and needs only Node.js (skip it if `node` isn't installed). Replace `DIAGRAM.drawio` with the actual filename:
    
    ```bash
    node -e '
    const fs = require("fs");
    const file = process.argv[1];
    let xml = fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8");
    const diagram = xml.match(/<mxfile[\s\S]*?<\/mxfile>|<mxGraphModel[\s\S]*?<\/mxGraphModel>/);
    if (diagram && xml.trim() !== diagram[0]) xml = diagram[0];
    xml = xml.replace(/="([^"]*)"/g, function(m, v)
    {
      return "=\"" + v.replace(/</g, "&lt;").replace(/&(?!(?:amp|lt|gt|quot|apos|#[0-9]+|#x[0-9a-fA-F]+);)/g, "&amp;") + "\"";
    });
    xml = xml.replace(/<mxCell id="([^"]+)" parent="\1"\s*\/>/g, "<mxCell id=\"$1\" parent=\"0\"/>");
    fs.writeFileSync(file, xml);
    ' "DIAGRAM.drawio"
    ```
    
    ## XML reference
    
    Before authoring XML, fetch and follow the condensed XML rules — skeleton, vertex and edge templates, labels, shapes, the layout grid, containers, swimlanes and edge styles:
    https://raw.githubusercontent.com/jgraph/drawio-mcp/main/shared/xml-essentials.md
    
    They cover most diagrams. Only when a diagram needs layers, tags, metadata and placeholders, tables, cross-functional grids or dark-mode colors, also fetch the full XML reference (it describes the draw.io MCP servers: for their `search_shapes` tool use the [lookup below](#shapes-and-icons), for `postLayout` / `routing` the CLI's [`--layout`](#elk-layout-for-xml)):
    https://raw.githubusercontent.com/jgraph/drawio-mcp/main/shared/xml-reference.md
    
    ### Shapes and icons
    
    When a diagram needs cloud, network or brand icons (AWS, Azure, GCP, Cisco, Kubernetes, GitHub, Slack…), never guess `mxgraph.*` names: look them all up in one call to `scripts/find-shapes.js` in this skill's folder (Node.js 18+, network), one query per icon, narrowed by library (`aws4`, `azure2`, `cisco19`). It searches draw.io's shape libraries, then its icon service, and prints `title width×height style` lines; use a style as is:
    
    ```bash
    node <skill-folder>/scripts/find-shapes.js "aws4 lambda" "github" "slack"
    ```

        p=Path('system-context-diagram.drawio.xml')
        before=p.read_text()

        changes={}

         match=re.search(pattern,before)

        title.set('value','<b>Draw &amp; Guess Game<br>(System Context)</b>')
        g=title.find('mxGeometry'); g.set('x','70'); g.set('y','5'); g.set('width','300'); g.set('height','35'); save(title)
        google=copy.deepcopy(objects['GGAcaCNyiWMHBxoHMokF-6'])
        google.set('id','c4-context-google-identity'); google.set('c4Name','Google Identity'); google.set('c4Description','Provides Google sign-in to authenticate users.'); google.set('label',game.get('label'))
        google.find('mxCell').set('parent','1'); google.find('mxCell').set('style',google.find('mxCell').get('style').replace('fontSize=9;',''))
        position(google,590,290,200,135)
        edge_style='edgeStyle=none;rounded=0;html=1;strokeColor=#707070;strokeWidth=2;fontColor=#707070;dashed=1;endArrow=block;endFill=1;fontSize=10;labelBackgroundColor=#ffffff;'
        label='<div style="text-align:center"><b>%c4Description%</b></div>'
        def edge(o,source,target,description,anchors,points=None):
         o.set('c4Description',description); o.attrib.pop('c4Technology',None); o.set('label',label)
         c=o.find('mxCell'); c.set('parent','1'); c.set('source',source); c.set('target',target); c.set('style',edge_style+anchors)
         for child in list(c): c.remove(child)
         g=E.SubElement(c,'mxGeometry',{'relative':'1','as':'geometry'})
         if points:
          a=E.SubElement(g,'Array',{'as':'points'})
          for x,y in points: E.SubElement(a,'mxPoint',{'x':str(x),'y':str(y)})
         return o
        pe=edge(objects['EwXlGVnZ-lc-afI8sIJ3-1'],player.get('id'),game.get('id'),'Registers, signs in,<br>joins rooms, plays games,<br>and views results','exitX=0.5;exitY=1;entryX=0.18;entryY=0;')
        he=edge(objects['EwXlGVnZ-lc-afI8sIJ3-3'],host.get('id'),game.get('id'),'Creates rooms, selects<br>the game mode,<br>and starts games','exitX=0.5;exitY=1;entryX=0.9;entryY=0;')
        save(pe); save(he)

        after=before

        cut=after.rfind('</root>',start,end)

        for original in E.fromstring(before).findall('diagram')[0].find('mxGraphModel/root'):

        for original,current in zip(E.fromstring(before).findall('diagram')[1:],new.findall('diagram')[1:]):

        ids=[x.get('id') for x in nd.iter() if x.get('id')]
        assert len(ids)==len(set(ids))
        for c in nd.iter('mxCell'):
         for key in ('parent','source','target'):
          assert not c.get(key) or c.get(key) in ids

        from pathlib import Path
        import re, xml.etree.ElementTree as E
        p=Path('system-context-diagram.drawio.xml'); before=p.read_text(); r=E.fromstring(before)
        d=r.findall('diagram')[0]; after=before
        texts={
         'lnmgxQ-TkLNhtuKLghFE-3':'A registered user who<br>joins rooms, plays Normal<br>and Imposter modes,<br>draws, guesses, votes<br>when applicable, and<br>views results.',
         'EwXlGVnZ-lc-afI8sIJ3-2':'A registered player who<br>creates rooms, selects<br>the game mode, and<br>starts games.<br>Also participates<br>as a player.'}
        for ident,description in texts.items():
         o=next(x for x in d.findall('.//object') if x.get('id')==ident)
         o.set('c4Description',description)
         o.set('label','<div style="font-size:11px;line-height:12px;text-align:center"><b>%c4Name%</b><br>[%c4Type%]<div style="margin-top:5px">%c4Description%</div></div>')
         c=o.find('mxCell'); c.set('style',c.get('style').replace('whitespace=wrap;','whiteSpace=wrap;')+'fontSize=11;overflow=hidden;spacingLeft=6;spacingRight=6;')
         pattern=rf'<object\b[^>]*\bid="{re.escape(ident)}"[^>]*>[\s\S]*?</object>'
         after,n=re.subn(pattern,lambda m:E.tostring(o,encoding='unicode').rstrip(),after,count=1); assert n==1
        new=E.fromstring(after)
        oldroot=E.fromstring(before).findall('diagram')[0].find('mxGraphModel/root'); newroot=new.findall('diagram')[0].find('mxGraphModel/root')
        for old in oldroot:
         if old.get('id') not in texts:
          current=next(x for x in newroot if x.get('id')==old.get('id'))
          assert E.tostring(old)==E.tostring(current),old.get('id')
        assert before[before.index('</diagram>'):]==after[after.index('</diagram>'):]
        p.write_text(after)
        print('Updated only Player and Host labels/styles. Legend, geometry, relationships and other pages unchanged.')

        from pathlib import Path
        import re, xml.etree.ElementTree as E
        p=Path('system-context-diagram.drawio.xml'); before=p.read_text(); old=E.fromstring(before); root=old.find('diagram/mxGraphModel/root'); after=before
        positions={'lnmgxQ-TkLNhtuKLghFE-13':450,'c4-context-google-identity':450,'GGAcaCNyiWMHBxoHMokF-1':820}
        for ident,y in positions.items():
         tag='mxCell' if ident=='GGAcaCNyiWMHBxoHMokF-1' else 'object'
         pattern=rf'<{tag}\b[^>]*\bid="{re.escape(ident)}"[^>]*>[\s\S]*?</{tag}>'
         match=re.search(pattern,after); assert match
         block=match.group(); updated,n=re.subn(r'(<mxGeometry\b[^>]*\by=")[^"]*(")',lambda m:m.group(1)+str(y)+m.group(2),block,count=1); assert n==1
         after=after.replace(block,updated,1)
        new=E.fromstring(after); nr=new.find('diagram/mxGraphModel/root')
        for orig in root:
         current=next(x for x in nr if x.get('id')==orig.get('id'))
         if orig.get('id') in positions:
          a=orig.find('mxGeometry') if orig.tag=='mxCell' else orig.find('mxCell/mxGeometry')
          b=current.find('mxGeometry') if current.tag=='mxCell' else current.find('mxCell/mxGeometry')
          a.set('y',b.get('y'))
         assert E.tostring(orig)==E.tostring(current),orig.get('id')
        assert before[before.index('</diagram>'):]==after[after.index('</diagram>'):]
        p.write_text(after)
        print('Spacing updated: 220px between people and systems; 235px between systems and legend. Only three Y coordinates changed.')

I propose three containers inside the **Draw & Guess Game** boundary:

| Container | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| **Game Client** | Displays registration, sign-in, lobby, drawing canvas, guesses, voting, and results. Shows each player only the information they are allowed to see. |
| **Game Server** | Authenticates users, manages rooms, selects keywords, assigns hidden roles, synchronizes drawing and turns, processes guesses and votes, and determines scores and winners. |
| **Game Database** | Stores user profiles, the default keyword pool, and any retained match results. |

**Player** and **Host** interact with the Game Client. **Google Identity** remains an external software system.

The connections would be:

| From → To | Purpose |
|---|---|
| Player / Host → Game Client | Plays games / hosts rooms |
| Game Client → Game Server | Sends account, room, and gameplay actions; receives game updates |
| Game Client → Google Identity | Initiates Google sign-in |
| Game Server → Google Identity | Validates Google sign-in |
| Game Server → Game Database | Reads and writes persistent game data |

The server should keep the keyword and imposter assignment authoritative, sending secret information only to eligible players. Normal and Imposter logic belong inside the same server unless you have a reason to deploy them separately.

Two details will determine the final containers and connection labels:

1. Is the client a **Windows desktop app, web app, or mobile app**?
2. What technology does your project require—for example, **C# WinForms with TCP sockets**, or another stack?

## Assistant

For your **WinForms + ASP.NET Core Minimal API** project, I propose **three containers** inside the **Draw & Guess Game** system boundary.

| Container | Technology | Responsibility |
|---|---|---|
| **Game Client** | C# / Windows Forms | Provides registration, sign-in, room lobby, drawing canvas, guessing, voting, and results screens. Displays the keyword only when permitted by the server. |
| **Game Server** | C# / ASP.NET Core Minimal API + SignalR | Manages authentication, rooms, keyword selection, turns, hidden roles, drawing synchronization, guesses, votes, scoring, and results. |
| **Game Database** | Relational database — engine to be confirmed | Stores accounts, Google identity links, the default keyword pool, and retained match results. |

The server hosts both HTTP endpoints and SignalR hubs **in the same application**, so they belong in one container. SignalR supports .NET clients and can run alongside ASP.NET Core APIs. [Microsoft documentation](https://dotnet.microsoft.com/en-us/apps/aspnet/signalr)

**Player**, **Host**, and **Google Identity** remain outside the system boundary. Host is still a Player with additional room controls.

| From → To | Relationship | Communication |
|---|---|---|
| Player → Game Client | Registers, signs in, joins rooms, and plays games | Desktop UI |
| Host → Game Client | Creates rooms, selects modes, and starts games | Desktop UI |
| Game Client → Game Server | Sends account and room requests | HTTPS / JSON |
| Game Client → Game Server | Exchanges drawing strokes, guesses, votes, and game updates | SignalR over HTTPS |
| Game Server → Game Database | Reads and writes persistent data | Database protocol |
| Game Client → Google Identity | Initiates Google sign-in through the system browser | OAuth 2.0 / OpenID Connect |
| Game Server → Google Identity | Obtains or validates Google identity information | HTTPS / OpenID Connect |

The **Game Server controls all game decisions**. In Imposter mode, it selects the keyword, assigns the imposter, sends the keyword only to non-imposters, manages turns and voting, and determines the winner. Active rooms and match state can initially live in server memory; restarting the server would end active matches.

For the drawing, I’ll put the actors above the WinForms client, the server below it, the database below the server, and Google Identity to the right. The legend will sit well below the main diagram, with generous space for descriptions and relationship labels.

**This is the proposed container design for review before editing.** The only unspecified technology is the database engine; we can label it “Relational database” until you choose one.

Use these technology labels on the Container diagram’s relationships:

| Game Client ↔ Game Server — real-time gameplay | **SignalR / WebSockets over TLS** |

Two details:

- **SignalR** can fall back to other transports, so **“SignalR / HTTPS”** is the more general label unless we explicitly require WebSockets.
- **EF Core** is the server’s database access library, not the network protocol. We can include it in the relationship description after choosing the database.

For the person-to-client arrows, the technology label is optional; “Uses” and “Hosts games using” are sufficient.

        with ZipFile(p) as z:
         slides=sorted([x for x in z.namelist() if re.fullmatch(r'ppt/slides/slide\d+\.xml',x)], key=lambda s:int(re.search(r'(\d+)\.xml',s).group(1)))
         for s in slides:

## Assistant

Your **C4 System Context diagram is consistent with the requirements**:

- **Player** and **Host** represent the user roles.
- **Draw & Guess Game** covers both game modes.
- **Google Identity** represents external authentication.
- The keyword pool and game rules correctly remain inside the system.

Keep it at this level. WinForms, TCP, APIs, and databases belong in the Container diagram.

For your **third assignment—system architecture and concurrent connections—I suggest three containers** within the **Draw & Guess Game** boundary:

| Container | Technology | Description for the diagram |
|---|---|---|
| **Game Client** | C# / Windows Forms | Provides account, lobby, drawing, guessing, voting, and results screens. Exchanges game messages with the server. |
| **Game Server** | C# / ASP.NET Core Minimal API + TCP sockets | Manages authentication, concurrent client connections, rooms, both game modes, drawing synchronization, voting, and results. |
| **Game Database** | Relational database; engine pending | Stores accounts, Google identity links, the default keyword pool, and retained match results. |

**Player**, **Host**, and **Google Identity** stay outside the boundary. One Game Client box represents the application installed on multiple players’ computers.

Use these connections:

| From → To | Relationship | Technology |
|---|---|---|
| Player → Game Client | Plays games using | Windows Forms UI |
| Host → Game Client | Creates and manages rooms using | Windows Forms UI |
| Game Client → Game Server | Sends gameplay actions | Custom application protocol / TCP |
| Game Server → Game Client | Sends game updates and results | Custom application protocol / TCP |
| Game Client → Game Server | Registers, signs in, and obtains a game session | HTTPS / JSON |
| Game Client → Google Identity | Initiates Google sign-in through the system browser | OpenID Connect / HTTPS |
| Game Server → Google Identity | Completes or verifies Google authentication | OpenID Connect / HTTPS |
| Game Server → Game Database | Reads and writes persistent data | Database protocol; engine pending |

The **Minimal API endpoints and TCP listener run in the same server application**, so they share one container. This proposal uses custom TCP communication for all gameplay; it preserves your Minimal API choice for authentication.

The server owns the keyword, imposter assignment, turn order, votes, and winning-side calculation. Clients receive only information appropriate to their role.

Your assignment also needs a **separate concurrency diagram** showing:

1. A TCP listener accepting multiple clients.
2. An asynchronous receive loop and outbound queue for each connection.
3. Messages routed to the appropriate room.
4. A sequential processing queue per room, preventing conflicting updates.
5. Room updates broadcast to connected participants.
6. Disconnect handling and connection cleanup.

These are internal server mechanisms, so they should not become extra C4 containers. Packet layouts and individual message definitions remain with the network-stack teammate.

**No diagram changes applied.** This is the proposed architecture to approve before drawing; the database engine still needs selection.

## User

the separate concuurrency diagram is which part of c4 modeling

## Assistant

The concurrency diagram is **not one of C4’s four core levels**: Context, Container, Component, and Code.

For your assignment, use two complementary views:

| Diagram | Purpose |
|---|---|
| **C4 Container diagram** | Shows the WinForms client, Game Server, database, and their communication technologies. |
| **Server concurrency diagram** | Shows how the server handles multiple connections, receive loops, room queues, and broadcasts. This can be a UML activity diagram with swimlanes. |

C4 also offers a **Dynamic diagram**, which shows the ordered interactions for a particular scenario—for example, two players joining a room and receiving updates. It can illustrate concurrent behavior, but a swimlane activity diagram explains the server’s concurrency mechanism more clearly.

For your assigned **“sơ đồ đa luồng/đa kết nối”**, I suggest a **server concurrency activity diagram**, alongside the C4 Container diagram. You do not need to force it into a C4 level.

## User

give me finalized plans for these 2 diagram

## Assistant

We’ll create **two diagrams**: a C4 Container diagram for the application structure, and a server concurrency activity diagram for handling multiple clients.

### 1. C4 Container — Draw & Guess Game

Place **Player**, **Host**, and **Google Identity** outside the **Draw & Guess Game** system boundary. Inside, show:

| Container | Technology | Responsibility |
|---|---|---|
| **Game Client** | C# / Windows Forms | Account screens, lobby, drawing canvas, guessing, voting, and results. Represents multiple installed client instances. |
| **Game Server** | C# / ASP.NET Core Minimal API + TCP sockets | Authentication, concurrent connections, rooms, keyword selection, Normal and Imposter rules, drawing synchronization, scoring, voting, and results. |
| **Game Database** | Relational database — engine pending | Accounts, Google identity links, default keywords, and retained match results. |

Minimal API endpoints and the TCP listener run in **one server process**, so they remain one container.

| Connection | Label | Technology |
|---|---|---|
| Player → Client | Plays games using | Windows Forms UI |
| Host → Client | Creates rooms, selects modes, and starts games using | Windows Forms UI |
| Client → Server | Registers, signs in, and obtains a game session | HTTPS / JSON |
| Client → Server | Sends gameplay actions | Custom application protocol / TCP |
| Server → Client | Sends game updates and results | Custom application protocol / TCP |
| Client → Google Identity | Initiates Google sign-in through the system browser | OpenID Connect / HTTPS |
| Server → Google Identity | Completes or verifies Google authentication | OpenID Connect / HTTPS |
| Server → Database | Reads and writes persistent data | Database protocol — engine pending |

The server validates the game session when a TCP connection is established. It controls hidden information and sends the keyword only to eligible players.

**Layout:** actors above the Client; Server beneath the Client; Database below the Server; Google Identity to the right. Use separate, clearly spaced HTTP and TCP connections.

### 2. Server Concurrency — Multiple Clients and Rooms

Use a **UML activity diagram with swimlanes**, titled **Game Server — Concurrent Connection and Room Processing**.

| Swimlane | Activities |
|---|---|
| **TCP Listener** | Start listener → accept connection → create connection handler → immediately continue accepting connections. |
| **Connection Handler — one per client** | Validate session → asynchronously receive bytes → assemble complete messages → validate and route messages. Repeat until disconnected. |
| **Room Processor — one per room** | Queue incoming actions → process one action at a time → check permissions and game phase → update authoritative room state → produce recipient-specific events. |
| **Outbound Delivery — one queue per client** | Queue outgoing events → serialize and frame messages → asynchronously send them in order. |
| **Persistence** | Load required data and save completed results without blocking the TCP accept loop. |

Show **Client A and Client B** feeding the same room processor, and **Client C** feeding another room processor. This makes both behaviors explicit:

- Connections and different rooms operate concurrently.
- Actions within one room are processed sequentially to avoid conflicting state changes.

Also show:

- **Private delivery:** keyword and role information go only to eligible recipients.
- **Room broadcasts:** drawing updates, turns, and public results go to room participants.
- **Disconnect path:** cancel connection tasks, remove room membership, release resources, and notify the room.
- **Slow or failed sends:** bounded outbound queues prevent one client from blocking the room.

Label receive and send operations as **asynchronous tasks**, rather than implying a dedicated operating-system thread for every client. Detailed packet fields, ports, and message definitions remain in your teammate’s network-stack diagrams.

For both diagrams, I’ll use readable wrapped descriptions and generous spacing. The existing C4 Context page stays unchanged; legends remain well away from the main drawings. The concurrency diagram gets its own page and activity notation.

**Ready for approval before editing.** The database can remain labeled “Relational database” until its engine is selected.

    ## Diagram 1: C4 Container
    
    Page: update the existing `C4 Container` page.
    
    Title: **Draw & Guess Game — Container Diagram**
    
    System boundary: **Draw & Guess Game**.
    
    ### Containers inside the system boundary
    
    | Container | Technology | Responsibilities |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | Game Client | C# / Windows Forms | Account screens, lobby, drawing canvas, guessing, voting, and results. One box represents the client application installed on multiple computers. |
    | Game Server | C# / ASP.NET Core Minimal API + TCP sockets | Authentication, concurrent connections, rooms, keyword selection, Normal and Imposter rules, drawing synchronization, scoring, voting, and results. |
    | Game Database | Microsoft SQL Server | Stores accounts, Google identity links, the default keyword pool, and retained match results. |
    
    Minimal API endpoints and the custom TCP listener run in one server process;
    they are not separate containers. Replace the earlier SignalR proposal with a
    team-developed application protocol over TCP for gameplay.
    
    ### External elements
    
    - **Player**: registered user participating in games.
    - **Host**: a Player with room creation, mode selection, and game-start controls.
      Host also has the regular Player interactions.
    - **Google Identity**: external software system providing Google sign-in.
    
    ### Relationships
    
    | From | To | Label | Technology |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | Player | Game Client | Plays games using | Windows Forms UI |
    | Host | Game Client | Creates rooms, selects modes, and starts games using | Windows Forms UI |
    | Game Client | Game Server | Registers, signs in, and obtains a game session | HTTPS / JSON |
    | Game Client | Game Server | Sends gameplay actions | Custom application protocol / TCP |
    | Game Server | Game Client | Sends game updates and results | Custom application protocol / TCP |
    | Game Client | Google Identity | Initiates Google sign-in through the system browser | OpenID Connect / HTTPS |
    | Game Server | Google Identity | Completes or verifies Google authentication | OpenID Connect / HTTPS |
    | Game Server | Game Database | Reads and writes accounts, keywords, and match results | TDS over TLS |
    
    Server data access uses **EF Core with the SQL Server provider**. EF Core is a
    library within the server, not a separate container or network protocol. The
    TDS-over-TLS label specifies the intended encrypted SQL Server connection.
    
    The server validates a game session when the TCP connection is established.
    It owns authoritative room state, keyword selection, hidden roles, turn order,
    votes, and results. Secret keywords are delivered only to eligible players.
    Active rooms initially live in server memory; server restart ends active matches.
    The exact Google authentication flow and TCP message formats are implementation
    details to coordinate with their respective owners.
    
    ### Layout and notation
    
    - Use C4 Person, Container, Database, external Software System, Relationship,
      and system-boundary notation consistent with the existing legend.
    - Actors above the Client; Server below the Client; Database below the Server;
      Google Identity to the right.
    - Clearly separate and label the HTTP and TCP relationships.
    - Wrap descriptions with explicit line breaks and provide sufficient shape size.
    - Leave generous spacing between actors, containers, and relationship labels.
    - Keep the Container page legend well below the main diagram; preserve its
      examples, content, styles, and internal layout. Move the whole group only as
      needed to implement the agreed spacing.
    
    ## Diagram 2: Server concurrency activity diagram
    
    Page: add a separate `Server Concurrency` page.
    
    Title: **Game Server — Concurrent Connection and Room Processing**
    
    Notation: UML activity diagram with swimlanes. This is a supporting architecture
    view, not an additional C4 level or a C4 Container diagram.
    
    ### Swimlanes
    
    | Swimlane | Activities |
    | --- | --- |
    | TCP Listener | Start listener, accept a connection, create a connection handler, and immediately continue accepting connections. |
    | Connection Handler — one per client | Validate session, asynchronously receive bytes, assemble complete messages, validate and route actions; repeat until disconnected. |
    | Room Processor — one per room | Queue actions, process one at a time, check permissions and game phase, update authoritative state, and produce recipient-specific events. |
    | Outbound Delivery — one queue per client | Queue events, serialize and frame messages, and asynchronously send them in order. |
    | Persistence | Load required data and save completed results using EF Core asynchronous operations against Microsoft SQL Server. |
    
    ### Concurrency and lifecycle behavior
    
    1. Show Client A and Client B routed to Room 1, and Client C routed to Room 2.
    2. Connections and different rooms operate concurrently; each room processes
       state-changing actions sequentially to prevent conflicting updates.
    3. Show private delivery of keywords and roles, and room broadcasts of drawing
       updates, turns, and public results.
    4. Give each client an outbound queue and a single ordered send loop. Bound the
       queues so a slow client cannot indefinitely consume memory or block a room;
       signal overload to the disconnect path rather than silently dropping
       authoritative game events. The exact queue limit remains an implementation
       detail.
    5. On disconnect or connection failure, cancel connection tasks, remove room
       membership through the room's processing path, release resources, and notify
       remaining participants. Host transfer and reconnect policy are unspecified.
    6. Keep database work out of the accept loop. Preserve per-room sequencing for
       operations whose results affect room state; asynchronous I/O does not imply
       unrestricted parallel mutation of room state.
    7. Label receive and send loops as asynchronous tasks; do not imply a dedicated
       operating-system thread per client.
    
    Use activity/swimlane notation with readable labels. Do not reuse C4 shapes as
    activity nodes; provide a small activity-specific legend only if needed.

    - **Player**: registered user participating in games.
    - **Host**: a Player with room creation, mode selection, and game-start controls.
      Host also has the regular Player interactions.
    - **Google Identity**: external software system providing Google sign-in.
    
    ### Relationships
    
    | From | To | Label | Technology |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | Player | Game Client | Plays games using | Windows Forms UI |
    | Host | Game Client | Creates rooms, selects modes, and starts games using | Windows Forms UI |
    | Game Client | Game Server | Registers, signs in, and obtains a game session | HTTPS / JSON |
    | Game Client | Game Server | Sends gameplay actions | Custom application protocol / TCP |
    | Game Server | Game Client | Sends game updates and results | Custom application protocol / TCP |
    | Game Client | Google Identity | Initiates Google sign-in through the system browser | OpenID Connect / HTTPS |
    | Game Server | Google Identity | Completes or verifies Google authentication | OpenID Connect / HTTPS |
    | Game Server | Game Database | Reads and writes accounts, keywords, and match results | TDS over TLS |
    
    Server data access uses **EF Core with the SQL Server provider**. EF Core is a
    library within the server, not a separate container or network protocol. The
    TDS-over-TLS label specifies the intended encrypted SQL Server connection.
    
    The server validates a game session when the TCP connection is established.
    It owns authoritative room state, keyword selection, hidden roles, turn order,
    votes, and results. Secret keywords are delivered only to eligible players.
    Active rooms initially live in server memory; server restart ends active matches.
    The exact Google authentication flow and TCP message formats are implementation
    details to coordinate with their respective owners.
    
    ### Layout and notation
    
    - Use C4 Person, Container, Database, external Software System, Relationship,
      and system-boundary notation consistent with the existing legend.
    - Actors above the Client; Server below the Client; Database below the Server;
      Google Identity to the right.
    - Clearly separate and label the HTTP and TCP relationships.
    - Wrap descriptions with explicit line breaks and provide sufficient shape size.
    - Leave generous spacing between actors, containers, and relationship labels.
    - Keep the Container page legend well below the main diagram; preserve its
      examples, content, styles, and internal layout. Move the whole group only as
      needed to implement the agreed spacing.
    
    ## Preservation and verification for implementation
    
    - Follow `AGENTS.md`: leave the complete C4 Context page and its legend unchanged.
    - Preserve the existing C4 Component and C4 Class pages.
    - Update only C4 Container for this plan. Server Concurrency is a separate plan.
    - Validate XML, unique IDs, parent/source/target references, and edge geometry.

    - Inspect a rendered preview if a renderer is available; otherwise report that

    # Server concurrency design log
    
    Date: 2026-11-07
    
    Status: Finalized plans recorded before implementation. The user confirmed
    Microsoft SQL Server. These changes have not yet been applied to draw.io.
    
    Target file: `system-context-diagram.drawio.xml`
    
    ## Scope and requirements
    
    The user's assigned work is system architecture: the overall architecture and
    multithreading/multiple-connection diagrams. The existing C4 Context provides
    the actors, system scope, and Google Identity integration.
    
    Requirements source: `final-project-requirements/Đồ án môn học.pptx`, slides 2–3,
    and the user's supplied assignment screenshot.
    
    - C# GUI clients, with at least two clients supported concurrently.
    - Server-side database.
    - Team-developed network stack, documented ports and message structures, and
      multiple simultaneous connections. Internet connectivity earns bonus points.
    - WinForms client and ASP.NET Core Minimal API server, as specified by the user.
    - Microsoft SQL Server, explicitly selected by the user.
    - Registration and sign-in required, with Google sign-in supported.
    - Normal and Imposter game modes, using a system-provided default keyword pool.
    - In Imposter mode, all players except the imposter know the keyword. The system
      manages turns, voting, imposter reveal, and determination of the winning side.
    
    Packet structures, port assignments, and detailed message definitions belong to
    the network-stack teammate's diagrams; they are not designed in this change.
    
    Related plan: [C4 Container](c4-container-design-log.md).
    
    The server uses EF Core with the SQL Server provider for asynchronous persistence.
    The Minimal API endpoints and custom TCP listener share one server process.
    
    ## Server concurrency activity diagram
    
    Page: add a separate `Server Concurrency` page.
    
    Title: **Game Server — Concurrent Connection and Room Processing**
    
    Notation: UML activity diagram with swimlanes. This is a supporting architecture
    view, not an additional C4 level or a C4 Container diagram.
    
    ### Swimlanes
    
    | Swimlane | Activities |
    | --- | --- |
    | TCP Listener | Start listener, accept a connection, create a connection handler, and immediately continue accepting connections. |
    | Connection Handler — one per client | Validate session, asynchronously receive bytes, assemble complete messages, validate and route actions; repeat until disconnected. |
    | Room Processor — one per room | Queue actions, process one at a time, check permissions and game phase, update authoritative state, and produce recipient-specific events. |
    | Outbound Delivery — one queue per client | Queue events, serialize and frame messages, and asynchronously send them in order. |
    | Persistence | Load required data and save completed results using EF Core asynchronous operations against Microsoft SQL Server. |
    
    ### Concurrency and lifecycle behavior
    
    1. Show Client A and Client B routed to Room 1, and Client C routed to Room 2.
    2. Connections and different rooms operate concurrently; each room processes
       state-changing actions sequentially to prevent conflicting updates.
    3. Show private delivery of keywords and roles, and room broadcasts of drawing
       updates, turns, and public results.
    4. Give each client an outbound queue and a single ordered send loop. Bound the
       queues so a slow client cannot indefinitely consume memory or block a room;
       signal overload to the disconnect path rather than silently dropping
       authoritative game events. The exact queue limit remains an implementation
       detail.
    5. On disconnect or connection failure, cancel connection tasks, remove room
       membership through the room's processing path, release resources, and notify
       remaining participants. Host transfer and reconnect policy are unspecified.
    6. Keep database work out of the accept loop. Preserve per-room sequencing for
       operations whose results affect room state; asynchronous I/O does not imply
       unrestricted parallel mutation of room state.
    7. Label receive and send loops as asynchronous tasks; do not imply a dedicated
       operating-system thread per client.
    
    Use activity/swimlane notation with readable labels. Do not reuse C4 shapes as
    activity nodes; provide a small activity-specific legend only if needed.
    
    ## Preservation and verification for implementation
    
    - Follow `AGENTS.md`: leave the complete C4 Context page and its legend unchanged.
    - Preserve the existing C4 Component and C4 Class pages.
    - Add only Server Concurrency for this plan; preserve existing diagram pages.
    - Validate XML, unique IDs, parent/source/target references, and edge geometry.
    - Compare untouched pages and protected legend contents against the original.
    - Inspect a rendered preview if a renderer is available; otherwise report that
      visual confirmation remains necessary in draw.io.

        r=E.parse('system-context-diagram.drawio.xml').getroot()

         d=next(x for x in r if x.get('name')==name); cells={}

          if c is not None: cells[x.get('id')]=c
         def box(i):
          c=cells[i];g=c.find('mxGeometry'); x=float(g.get('x',0));y=float(g.get('y',0));parent=c.get('parent')
          if parent in cells and cells[parent].get('vertex')=='1':
           a,b,_,_=box(parent);x+=a;y+=b
          return x,y,float(g.get('width',0)),float(g.get('height',0))
         ids=[i for i,c in cells.items() if c.get('vertex')=='1' and not any(n.get('parent')==i for n in cells.values())]

        const fs = require('fs');const file='system-context-diagram.drawio.xml';const original=fs.readFileSync(file,'utf8');let xml=original;
        const diagram=xml.match(/<mxfile[\s\S]*?<\/mxfile>|<mxGraphModel[\s\S]*?<\/mxGraphModel>/);

        xml=xml.replace(/<mxCell id="([^"]+)" parent="\1"\s*\/>/g,'<mxCell id="$1" parent="0"/>');
        if(xml!==original) throw new Error('Unexpected repair needed');console.log('XML repair pass: no repairs needed.');

## User

server concurrency, what kind of approach does this diagram use, thread-per-connection or thread pool or event-driven + theread

## Assistant

The diagram uses **event-driven asynchronous I/O backed by the .NET thread pool**.

| Part | Approach |
|---|---|
| TCP listener | Asynchronously waits for new connections. |
| Each client connection | Has a receive task and an ordered send task. These tasks **do not own dedicated threads**. |
| Each room | Has a queue consumed sequentially, so only one action changes that room’s state at a time. |
| Different rooms | Can process actions concurrently using thread-pool threads. |
| Database access | Uses asynchronous EF Core operations. |

While a task awaits network or database I/O, it releases its executing thread. When work becomes ready, execution resumes on an available thread-pool thread.

The **room processing follows an actor-style approach**: each room owns its state and processes queued actions sequentially. A room does not require a dedicated thread either.

For your report, use:

> **An asynchronous, event-driven server using .NET thread-pool execution, with per-connection receive/send tasks and sequential message processing per room.**

This is **not thread-per-connection**. The important distinction is **one task per loop, rather than one thread per client**.

## User

does this use non-blocking API

## Assistant

Yes—the diagram assumes **asynchronous, non-blocking I/O APIs**, such as:

| Operation | C# API |
|---|---|
| Accept connections | `TcpListener.AcceptTcpClientAsync()` |
| Receive data | `NetworkStream.ReadAsync()` |
| Send data | `NetworkStream.WriteAsync()` |
| Wait for queued messages | `ChannelReader.ReadAsync()` |
| Access SQL Server | EF Core methods such as `ToListAsync()` and `SaveChangesAsync()` |

When these operations are pending, `await` suspends the task **without blocking a thread**. Processing resumes when the operation completes.

This describes the intended implementation. It remains non-blocking only if the code avoids synchronous waits such as `.Wait()`, `.Result`, and blocking socket calls.

## Activity
